Some days I’m quiet, or I don’t feel like talking much, but then something needs fixing or there’s mail to sort, and you just get on with it. My wife used to say I’d brood, but she said that about a lot of things.\n\nSo… I can’t say for sure. Nothing comes to mind clear like that.\n\nDoctor: Before this current three-month period, was there ever another time in your life when you felt this same kind of unusually high or energized mood?\n\nPatient: Another time like this? I don’t know… I’ve had times where I got busy, sure. When you own a place, there’s always something—gutters, paint, that one cabinet door that never hangs right. I used to get going on things and stay with them longer than I probably should’ve.\n\nBut the same kind of high or energized like you mean… I’m not sure. Nothing jumps out at me the way this did. It’s hard to compare, because when you’re younger you’ve got more steam anyway, you know? You can work all day and still mow the lawn after supper. That doesn’t mean it was the same thing.\n\nSo… I can’t really say I remember another time like this, no.\n\nDoctor: Before this current spell, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt this same kind of unusually high or energized mood?\n\nPatient: Before this spell? Well… I keep trying to think back, and it’s hard because when you’re younger you’ve got more energy anyway. I used to be able to haul lumber, patch a roof, then still go out and fool with the yard till dark. That old maple out back, I trimmed that myself once—shouldn’t have, probably.\n\nBut this kind of charged-up feeling… most days lately I feel almost joyful, like I’m young again, like there’s a motor in me. And then if my wife or one of the contractors questions what I’m doing, I get hot real quick. I start yelling before I even mean to.\n\nI don’t remember another time quite like that before this one.\n\nDoctor: Before this current episode, did you ever have another time in your life when you felt this same kind of unusually high or energized mood?\n\nPatient: I know you’ve asked that a couple times, and I’m trying to give you a straight answer. I mean, people have always had opinions about what I ought to do—my brothers, my wife, neighbors even. Everybody’s got advice when it’s not their house or their money.\n\nBut before this current spell… I’m trying to think. I had times I worked hard, got wound up with projects, sure. There was one summer I was fixing the back steps and then somehow ended up redoing half the shed, and my wife said I was overdoing it. But that’s just how you get things done, you know? If you stop, the weather changes, then the wood warps, and then you’ve got twice the problem.\n\nSo… serious problems? Other people thinking I needed help? I don’t really remember anything like that before. Most days I feel charged up, almost happy in a way, like I’m young again and could get things done like I used to.\n\nBut then somebody questions me—my wife, or those contractors—and I just get hot fast. I’ll be yelling before I even mean to. It’s like they poke at me and I’m already wound up.\n\nBefore this… I don’t know that I remember it feeling quite like this.\n\n[Last Question]\nBefore this current episode, did you ever have another period when you felt unusually high or energized like this?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 73 / not_in_workforce / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\nmanifestation: \"Most days I feel charged up and almost joyful, like I’m young again, but if my wife or the contractors question me, I get hot fast and start yelling.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Before this one? I keep trying to think back, but what I can tell you is how it feels now. Most days I feel charged up, almost joyful, like I’m young again—like I could get moving and do things the way I used to.

But then if my wife starts questioning me, or those contractors start acting like I don’t know what I’m talking about, I get hot fast. I mean, I’ll be yelling before I even realize I’m yelling. That’s the part people keep making such a big deal about.

    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n20\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF30.2, F31.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… they say I’m here because I spent our savings. On wood, lights, all the display things, you know, the kind you need if you’re going to do it right. And I kept working on it even after dark, because if I stopped then it felt like the whole house—maybe the whole block—was going to miss its chance.\n\nPeople act like it was just buying things, but it wasn’t like that to me. It was important. I mean, you ever see how a place looks when the lights hit just right? Reminds me of when I was younger and folks would drive slow past a good display. Anyway… that’s what they’re upset about.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you had a special ability or mission with the display that other people didn't understand?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I did feel that way. Not “special” like I’m better than everybody, but… I honestly think I can make that old Schiller Park house into something people will come see. Like a real thing, something important.\n\nPeople don’t understand light and pictures the way I do. They see extension cords and plywood and receipts, but I see how it’s supposed to look when the colors hit the windows, when you stand across the street. My uncle used to take us past houses like that when I was a kid—slow in the car, everybody quiet for a second—and I know how to make that happen. So yeah, maybe I thought I had something they weren’t seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, sleep’s been funny, but I can’t say it the way you’re asking. Some nights I’m up thinking about where things should go, and then I look at the clock and—well, clocks in that house never were right anyway, one in the kitchen always ran slow.\n\nSo… maybe I haven’t been paying attention to how much, exactly. I just know there’s a lot to do, and nighttime gets quiet, and that’s when you can see the lights better.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling unusually high, excited, or irritable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… high, I guess. Not like drugs or anything, I don’t mean that. Just charged up. Most days lately I wake up and I feel almost joyful, like I’m young again and I can see the whole thing coming together.\n\nBut then if my wife starts in, or the contractors look at me like I don’t know what I’m doing, I get hot fast. I’ll start yelling before I even mean to. It’s just—people ask the same questions over and over, and meanwhile there’s work to do. You know, when I was a kid, men just got things done, they didn’t stand around with clipboards… but yes, I’ve been more excited, and more irritable too.\n\nDoctor: Have you been more active than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose I have been. I can’t seem to just sit in the chair anymore. I’ll sit down and then I’m right back up—out to the garden, then into the darkroom, then onto the porch.\n\nI keep carrying tools around, fixing little things before anybody even asks me to. Like, if I see a loose board or a cord not sitting right, I’m already on it. My wife says I’m wearing a path through the house, but that old porch always did need attention… my father would’ve had a fit seeing it like that.\n\nSo yes, I’ve been moving around more. Hard to stay still when there’s work staring at you.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you were chosen for a special purpose with this display?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I know how that sounds. People already think I’m mixed up, but I’m telling you, this neighborhood was picked for it. For my last big civic art piece.\n\nIt’s not just me deciding that out of nowhere. The trucks slow down when they pass, and the neighbors look over like they know what’s happening. You can tell by little things like that. Folks don’t stop and stare for nothing. My father used to say a man’s work speaks before he does, and—well, that’s what this is. A purpose. A display people are meant to see.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, have you ever had a period where you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Depressed… I don’t know. I mean, everybody has bad stretches, don’t they? Especially when you get older and the house gets quiet in certain ways.\n\nBut most of the day, every day, two weeks… I’m not sure I could mark it off like that. I don’t keep a calendar for feelings. My wife might say I get moody, but she says a lot of things when she’s irritated with me. I can’t say for sure, doctor.\n\nDoctor: How many days in a row have you been feeling this charged up?\n\nPatient: Oh, days… it’s more than days, I guess. It’s been going on about three months now, since we started that remodeling and the whole house got turned upside down.\n\nOnce they started moving things around, nothing was where it belonged, and I couldn’t just leave it all like that. You know, a house has a rhythm to it, and when people come in tearing up walls and shifting boxes—well, somebody’s got to keep track of it. So… yeah, about three months.\n\nDoctor: At any time in your life, have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say it that cleanly. Two weeks is a funny thing to ask about when you’re looking back over a whole life, you know?\n\nThere were times I was low, sure, but near every day… I don’t keep track like that. Back when I was working—well, before I stopped—some mornings were harder than others, but then you get up anyway because that’s what you do. My wife always said I’d sit too long at the table with my coffee, but she notices everything.\n\nSo… I’m not sure, doctor. I can’t really give you a yes or no on that.\n\nDoctor: Before this time, have you ever had another period when you felt this unusually energized or revved up?\n\nPatient: Before this time? I don’t know… I mean, I’ve had times where I had more pep, sure, like when there was work to be done or when the garage needed clearing out. A man gets moving when something needs doing.\n\nBut “unusually energized,” that’s hard to pin down. My wife might say I get on a roll sometimes, but she says that if I reorganize one cabinet, you know? Then again, she never liked the way I kept the tools in coffee cans—said it made no sense, but I knew where every screw was.\n\nSo… another period like this? I can’t really say. Nothing comes to mind the same way, not clear enough for me to tell you yes.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices or seeing things that other people could not?\n\nPatient: Voices or seeing things… I don’t know. I mean, sometimes you hear the house settling, especially with all that work going on, pipes knocking, boards creaking, and everybody acts like you’re imagining it. But houses make sounds, they always have.\n\nSeeing things other people couldn’t… I don’t think so. Unless you mean like noticing something out of place that nobody else catches. My wife can walk right past a crooked picture frame and it’ll bother me all day. But that’s not the same thing, I guess.\n\nSo… no, not that I can clearly say.\n\nDoctor: At any point during these past three months, did you still believe you had this special purpose with the display when you were not feeling especially charged up or excited?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so… not before I got all wound up about the remodeling and the display and what it was supposed to be. Before that, trucks were just trucks, neighbors were just looking because people look, you know? Folks always look when there’s work being done on a house.\n\nIt was only once I felt like I had to make the place into something bigger—like a project for everyone, not just my house—that those things started to seem like signs. Before that, I wasn’t reading all that into it.\n\nDoctor: At any time in your life, have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Two weeks… depressed nearly every day? I don’t know if I’d call it that. I’ve had low stretches, sure. Everybody does, especially when you get older and things don’t move like they used to.\n\nBut two weeks straight, every day… I’m not sure. I don’t really keep track like that. Some days I’m quiet, or I don’t feel like talking much, but then something needs fixing or there’s mail to sort, and you just get on with it. My wife used to say I’d brood, but she said that about a lot of things.\n\nSo… I can’t say for sure. Nothing comes to mind clear like that.\n\nDoctor: Before this current three-month period, was there ever another time in your life when you felt this same kind of unusually high or energized mood?\n\nPatient: Another time like this? I don’t know… I’ve had times where I got busy, sure. When you own a place, there’s always something—gutters, paint, that one cabinet door that never hangs right. I used to get going on things and stay with them longer than I probably should’ve.\n\nBut the same kind of high or energized like you mean… I’m not sure. Nothing jumps out at me the way this did. It’s hard to compare, because when you’re younger you’ve got more steam anyway, you know? You can work all day and still mow the lawn after supper. That doesn’t mean it was the same thing.\n\nSo… I can’t really say I remember another time like this, no.\n\nDoctor: Before this current spell, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt this same kind of unusually high or energized mood?\n\nPatient: Before this spell? Well… I keep trying to think back, and it’s hard because when you’re younger you’ve got more energy anyway. I used to be able to haul lumber, patch a roof, then still go out and fool with the yard till dark. That old maple out back, I trimmed that myself once—shouldn’t have, probably.\n\nBut this kind of charged-up feeling… most days lately I feel almost joyful, like I’m young again, like there’s a motor in me. And then if my wife or one of the contractors questions what I’m doing, I get hot real quick. I start yelling before I even mean to.\n\nI don’t remember another time quite like that before this one.\n\nDoctor: Before this current episode, did you ever have another time in your life when you felt this same kind of unusually high or energized mood?\n\nPatient: I know you’ve asked that a couple times, and I’m trying to give you a straight answer. Before this? I don’t really remember another time that felt the same.\n\nWhat I’ve got now is… most days I feel charged up, almost joyful, like I’m young again. Like I can take on things I haven’t done in years. But then if my wife, or one of those contractors, starts questioning me—asking why I’m doing this or that—I get hot fast. I mean, I’m yelling before I’ve even thought it through.\n\nI don’t recall being quite like that before. Maybe I had energy when I was younger, but this feels different.\n\nDoctor: Before this current spell, was there ever another time in your life when you became so revved up or energized that it caused serious problems or other people thought you needed help?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say it ever caused serious problems before, not like you’re putting it. I mean, people have always had opinions about what I ought to do—my brothers, my wife, neighbors even. Everybody’s got advice when it’s not their house or their money.\n\nBut before this current spell… I’m trying to think. I had times I worked hard, got wound up with projects, sure. There was one summer I was fixing the back steps and then somehow ended up redoing half the shed, and my wife said I was overdoing it. But that’s just how you get things done, you know? If you stop, the weather changes, then the wood warps, and then you’ve got twice the problem.\n\nSo… serious problems? Other people thinking I needed help? I don’t really remember anything like that before. Not that stands out.\n\nDoctor: Before this current episode, was there ever a time in your life when you were hospitalized for feeling unusually high, energized, or out of control?\n\nPatient: Hospitalized? No, I don’t think so. Not for that.\n\nI’ve been in hospitals for other ordinary things over the years—when you get to my age, seems like everybody’s got some appointment or somebody’s checking something—but not because I was too energized or out of control. Nobody ever hauled me in for being worked up like that, not that I remember.\n\nI mean, my wife has told me plenty of times I need to slow down, but that’s not the same as a hospital.\n\nDoctor: Before this current episode, was there ever another time in your life when you felt this same kind of unusually high or energized mood?\n\nPatient: I know, I know, you’re asking before this time. I’m trying to sort that out, but what keeps coming to mind is how I am now. Most days I feel charged up, almost happy in a way, like I’m young again and could get things done like I used to.\n\nBut then somebody questions me—my wife, or those contractors—and I just get hot fast. Most days I feel charged up, almost joyful, like I’m young again—like I could get moving and do things the way I used to.\n\nBut then if my wife starts questioning me, or those contractors start acting like I don’t know what I’m talking about, I get hot fast. I don’t know… I’ve had times where I got busy, sure. When you own a place, there’s always something—gutters, paint, that one cabinet door that never hangs right. I used to get going on things and stay with them longer than I probably should’ve.\n\nBut the same kind of high or energized like you mean… I’m not sure. Nothing jumps out at me the way this did. It’s hard to compare, because when you’re younger you’ve got more steam anyway, you know? You can work all day and still mow the lawn after supper. That doesn’t mean it was the same thing.\n\nSo… I can’t really say I remember another time like this, no.\n\nDoctor: Before this current spell, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt this same kind of unusually high or energized mood?\n\nPatient: Before this spell? Well… I keep trying to think back, and it’s hard because when you’re younger you’ve got more energy anyway. I used to be able to haul lumber, patch a roof, then still go out and fool with the yard till dark. That old maple out back, I trimmed that myself once—shouldn’t have, probably.\n\nBut this kind of charged-up feeling… most days lately I feel almost joyful, like I’m young again, like there’s a motor in me. And then if my wife or one of the contractors questions what I’m doing, I get hot real quick. I start yelling before I even mean to.\n\nI don’t remember another time quite like that before this one.\n\nDoctor: Before this current episode, did you ever have another time in your life when you felt this same kind of unusually high or energized mood?\n\nPatient: I know you’ve asked that a couple times, and I’m trying to give you a straight answer. Before this? I don’t really remember another time that felt the same.\n\nWhat I’ve got now is… most days I feel charged up, almost joyful, like I’m young again. Like I can take on things I haven’t done in years. But then if my wife, or one of those contractors, starts questioning me—asking why I’m doing this or that—I get hot fast. I mean, I’m yelling before I’ve even thought it through.\n\nI don’t recall being quite like that before. Maybe I had energy when I was younger, but this feels different.\n\nDoctor: Before this current spell, was there ever another time in your life when you became so revved up or energized that it caused serious problems or other people thought you needed help?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say it ever caused serious problems before, not like you’re putting it. I mean, people have always had opinions about what I ought to do—my brothers, my wife, neighbors even. Everybody’s got advice when it’s not their house or their money.\n\nBut before this current spell… I’m trying to think. I had times I worked hard, got wound up with projects, sure. There was one summer I was fixing the back steps and then somehow ended up redoing half the shed, and my wife said I was overdoing it. But that’s just how you get things done, you know? If you stop, the weather changes, then the wood warps, and then you’ve got twice the problem.\n\nSo… serious problems? Other people thinking I needed help? I don’t really remember anything like that before. Not that stands out.\n\nDoctor: Before this current episode, was there ever a time in your life when you were hospitalized for feeling unusually high, energized, or out of control?\n\nPatient: Hospitalized? No, I don’t think so. Not for that.\n\nI’ve been in hospitals for other ordinary things over the years—when you get to my age, seems like everybody’s got some appointment or somebody’s checking something—but not because I was too energized or out of control. Nobody ever hauled me in for being worked up like that, not that I remember.\n\nI mean, my wife has told me plenty of times I need to slow down, but that’s not the same as a hospital.\n\nDoctor: Before this current episode, was there ever another time in your life when you felt this same kind of unusually high or energized mood?\n\nPatient: I know, I know, you’re asking before this time. I’m trying to sort that out, but what keeps coming to mind is how I am now. Most days I feel charged up, almost happy in a way, like I’m young again and could get things done like I used to.\n\nBut then somebody questions me—my wife, or those contractors—and I just get hot fast. I’ll be yelling before I even mean to. It’s like they poke at me and I’m already wound up.\n\nBefore this… I don’t know that I remember it feeling quite like this.\n\nDoctor: Before this current episode, did you ever have another period when you felt unusually high or energized like this?\n\nPatient: Before this one? I keep trying to think back, but what I can tell you is how it feels now. Most days I feel charged up, almost joyful, like I’m young again—like I could get moving and do things the way I used to.\n\nBut then if my wife starts questioning me, or those contractors start acting like I don’t know what I’m talking about, I get hot fast. I mean, I’ll be yelling before I even realize I’m yelling. That’s the part people keep making such a big deal about."
